L1927: ## 21. Labeling Summary of Considerations and Key Additional Information
L1928: ## USPI Labeling Review
L1929: ## Overview of Major Labeling Changes:
L1930: - Information highlighted below are significant changes made to the prescribing information from the Applicant's proposed label submitted on October 4, 2019 for INMAZEB (atoltivimab, maftivimab, and odesivimab-ebgn) with the to-be-approved USPI.
- HIGHLIGHTS and TABLE OF CONTENTS were revised for consistency with the full Prescribing Information.
L1931: ## FULL PRESCRIBING INFORMATION
L1932: ## 1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE
L1933: - The indications statement was modified to add “including neonates born to a mother who is RT-PCR positive for *Zaire ebolavirus* infection” because the proposed indication in adults and pediatric patients was non-specific for pediatric age group. Refer to Section II.6.4.4 for additional details.
- The following limitations of use was added following precedent with influenza labeling, which has similar Limitation of Use for viral infections that can change over time:“*Zaire ebolavirus* can change over time, and factors such as emergence of resistance, or changes in viral virulence could diminish the clinical benefit of antiviral drugs. Consider available information on drug susceptibility patterns for circulating *Zaire ebolavirus* strains when deciding whether to use INMAZEB.”
L1934: ## 2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION
L1935: ## 2.1 Recommended Dosage
L1936: - minimize medication errors the recommendation dosage was revised

Therefore, to
L1937: ## 2.2 Preparation and Administration
L1938: - Additional details were added to preparation and administration instructions to mitigate potential medication errors by providing clear instructions on how to prepare and administer INMAZEB. Additionally, to aid infusion preparation, the volume conversion of 3 mL/kg was added.
- Table 1 INMAZEB Infusion Volumes and Time by Body Weight, was added to provide clear direction on prepared infusion volume and infusion time based on the patient's
L1939: weight down to 0.5 kg due to issues with endotoxin. Refer to Section II.6.4.4 and II.7.7.3 for additional details.
L1940: ## 5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS
L1941: ## 5.1 Hypersensitivity Reactions Including Infusion-Associated Events
L1942: This warning was revised to add infusion-associated events during and post-infusion with INMAZEB and recommendation to slow or interrupt infusion of INMAZEB if the patient develops any signs of infusion-associated events or other adverse events. Refer to Section I.1 and II.7.7.2 for additional details.
L1943: ## 6 ADVERSE REACTIONS
L1944: ## 6.1 Clinical Trials Experience
L1945: (b) (4) was deleted to focus the safety findings from the PALM trial. The common adverse reactions, including adverse events that occurred during INMAZEB infusion in \ge 10\% of adult and pediatric subjects, discontinuation and infusion rate adjustment, and selected laboratory abnormalities sections summarized data from the PALM trial comparing INMAZEB to an investigational control. It was noted that there is additional safety information in 228 subjects (190 adult subjects and 38 pediatric subjects) from the expanded access program but only the safety data from PALM trial is presented in Section 6. Refer to Section II.7.6 for additional details.
L1946: ## 7 DRUG INTERACTIONS
L1947: ## 7.1 Vaccine Interactions
L1948: The following language recommending avoiding concurrent administration of live vaccine during treatment with INMAZEB was added. Refer to Section II.8.2 for additional details.
L1949: “No vaccine-therapeutic interaction studies have been performed in human subjects using INMAZEB. However, because of the potential for INMAZEB to inhibit replication of a live vaccine virus indicated for prevention of *Zaire ebolavirus* infection and possibly reduce the efficacy of the vaccine, avoid the concurrent administration of a live vaccine during treatment with INMAZEB. The interval between live vaccination following initiation of INMAZEB therapy should be in accordance with current vaccination guidelines. The efficacy of INMAZEB among subjects who reported receipt of a recombinant live vaccine prior to their enrollment in the PALM clinical trial was similar to subjects who did not receive a vaccine.”
L1950: ## 8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
L1951: ## 8.1 Pregnancy
L1952: ## Risk Summary
L1953: Summary of high rate of maternal and fetal/neonatal morbidity and mortality associated with underlying maternal *Zaire ebolavirus* infection was added based on PALM trial, expanded access program, and published literature. Refer to Section II.8.4 for additional details.
L1954: ## Clinical Considerations
L1955: The following statement, “Treatment should not be withheld due to pregnancy” was added. Refer to Section II.8.4 for additional details.
L1956: ## 8.2 Lactation
L1957: The following statement, “The effects of local gastrointestinal exposure and limited systemic exposure in the breastfed infant to atoltivimab, maftivimab, or odesivimab are unknown” was added. Refer to Section II.8.4 for additional details.
L1958: ## 8.4 Pediatric Use
L1959: This section was revised to state the safety and effectiveness of INMAZEB for the treatment of infection caused by *Zaire Ebola virus* have been established in pediatric patients birth to less than 18 years of age based on data from 39 pediatric subjects, including neonates born to a mother who is RT-PCR positive for *Zaire Ebola virus* based on the PALM trial. The 28-day mortality and safety in adults and pediatric subjects were similar. Refer to Section II.6.4.4 and II.8.3 for additional details.
L1960: ## 12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L1961: ## 12.4 Microbiology
L1962: Labeling changes to this section predominantly were made to add additional details pertaining the mechanism of action and antiviral activity. (b) (4) were removed due to conflicting data in the BLA submission.
L1963: ## Antiviral Activity
L1964: The following statements were added, “Atoltivimab and odesivimab did not demonstrate any neutralizing activity in Mayinga, Kikwit, and Makona strains of *Zaire ebolavirus*” and “The EC50 values of atoltivimab and odesivimab were 2.9 nM and 1.6 nM, respectively, whereas maftivimab did not exhibit any Fc\gammaRIIIa signaling activity at the maximum concentration tested, 40 nM.” Refer to Section II.7.7.1 for additional details.
L1965: ## Resistance
L1966: The following statement was added, “No clinical data are available on the development of EBOV resistance to INMAZEB. The cell culture development of EBOV resistance to INMAZEB has not been assessed to date. A GP_E280G amino acid substitution identified by routine surveillance in the Democratic Republic of the Congo resulted in a loss of neutralization activity of at least 134-fold mediated by the single human monoclonal antibody atoltivimab in a lentivirus-based pseudovirus system. A GP_E564K substitution identified in an infected NHP PK study resulted in a loss of neutralization activity of at least 215-fold mediated by the single human monoclonal antibody maftivimab in a lentivirus-based pseudovirus system. The clinical significance of these substitutions is unknown.” Refer to Section II.7.7.1 for additional details.
L1967: ## Immune Response
L1968: The following statement was added, “Interaction studies with recombinant live EBOV vaccines and INMAZEB have not been conducted.”
L1969: ## 13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L1970: ## 13.2 Animal Toxicology and/or Pharmacology
L1971: (b) (4) was removed because there were no clinically relevant safety concerns. Refer to Section III.13.1.4 for additional details.
L1972: ## 14 CLINICAL STUDIES
L1973: - Description of the PALM trial was revised to add:Enrolled subjects had “documented” *Zaire Ebola virus*, confirmed with RT-PCR
- Subject demographics was revised to add “neonates born to a mother who is cleared *Zaire ebolavirus* following a course of her assigned investigational medication were also eligible to be enrolled at investigator discretion regarding the likelihood that the neonate was infected.”
- Efficacy results were revised present 28-day mortality, [REDACTED](b) (4). Twenty-eight-day mortality was noted as the pre-specified primary efficacy endpoint in the PALM trial, and the primary analysis population was described. Mortality rates in Table 7 were revised to present efficacy results by pediatric age groups and sex. Refer to Section II for additional details.
- Kaplan-Meier curve was revised to show overall mortality [REDACTED](b) (4). Refer to Section III.16.3 for additional details.
- [REDACTED](b) (4) was removed because the efficacy results were already presented in the main efficacy table. Refer to Section III.16.4 for additional details.
- In addition, refer to Section II.6.4 and III.16 for additional details on efficacy results.
L1974: ## 22. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1975: Below are the agreed upon PMRs (Table 115) and PMCs (Table 116) for this application.
L1976: **Table 115. Agreed Postmarketing Requirements**
L1977: PMR	Milestones
1. Conduct a phenotypic study to determine the impact on binding and antiviral activity against REGN-EB3, atoltivimab (REGN3470), odesivimab (REGN3471), and maftivimab (REGN3479) using lentivirus-based particles pseudotyped with EBOV GP containing these substitutions to determine shifts in susceptibility: I274M, W275L, G528R, I544T, H549R, N563T, and E564A.	Study Completion: 02/2021
Final Report Submission: 05/2021
2. Conduct a study to characterize genotypically/phenotypically known atoltivimab (REGN3470), odesivimab (REGN3471), and maftivimab (REGN3479) resistant variants and those identified through VSV-based chimeric virus cell culture escape studies with respect to binding, neutralization, and ADCC assays with the individual mAbs (REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN3479) and the REGN-EB3 mAb combination (INMAZEB). Specifically assess cross-resistance between atoltivimab (REGN3470) and odesivimab (REGN3471) resistant variants using an ADCC assay.	Study/Trial Completion: 09/2022
Final Report Submission: 03/2023
L1978: **Table 116. Agreed Postmarketing Commitments**
L1979: PMC	Milestones
3. Submit a final report with complete, unblinded safety data for all subjects who were enrolled after interim results of the initial phase of the PALM trial and were treated with atoltivimab, maftivimab, and odesivimab-ebgn (REGN-EB3) for Zaire ebolavirus infection during the PALM Extension Phase.	Final Report Submission: 03/2022
4. Define the precise epitopes of atoltivimab (REGN3470), odesivimab (REGN3471), and maftivimab (REGN3479).	Study/Trial Completion: 12/2020
Final Report Submission: 04/2021
5. Perform complete resistance analysis of sequences derived from subjects treated with REGN-EB3 in the PALM trial, if access to the data becomes available.	Final Report Submission: 09/2022
6. Collaborate with US public health agencies, other public health agencies and local health authorities, as appropriate to design and conduct a trial to evaluate the efficacy, safety and pharmacokinetics of a higher dose of INMAZEB (atoltivimab, maftivimab, and odesivimab-ebgn) vs. INMAZEB 150 mg/kg in Zaire ebolavirus-infected adult and pediatric patients with cycle threshold (CT) values for nucleoprotein targets of less than or equal to 22 to determine if a change in dosing regimen is needed in these patients.	Draft Protocol Submission: 05/2021
Final Protocol Submission: To be determined when such trial is feasible
7. Re-evaluate and update REGN-EB3 drug substance (DS) and drug product lot release and stability specifications based on lots manufactured by the [REDACTED] (b) (4) DS processes. The corresponding data, the analysis, and updated specifications will be submitted with the PAS for the registration of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) commercial manufacturing process.	Final Report Submission: 01/2021
8. Conduct a real-world Transport Qualification study that includes a product quality assessment using REGN-EB3 drug product. The real-world Transport Qualification study results will be submitted in a final report to the BLA.	Final Report Submission: 08/2021
9. Re-evaluate and optimize the manufacture, qualification and stability controls used to ensure the performance of Ebola Virus-Like Particles (VLPs) in the pseudovirus neutralization assays. The re-evaluation, development study results and the final control strategy for Ebola VLPs will be provided in the final report to the BLA.	Final Report Submission: 12/2021
10. Provide microbial hold time data in a microbial challenge study to support the total in-use time (storage and infusion time) of diluted INMAZEB in 5% Dextrose Injection beyond 4-hours at ambient temperature. The study should be conducted for twice the worst-case in-use time and bracketing the drug product concentrations that would be administered to patients. The study should also be representative of the in-use conditions; for example, neonates may be kept at temperatures above 20-25°C during infusion and the higher temperatures should be simulated in the study supporting in-use conditions.	Final Report Submission: 04/2021

L2063: Figure 9 displays three graphs showing the effect of GP sequence variants (Zaire 2014, E280G, and E564K) on Virus-Like Particle (VLP) neutralization. The Y-axis represents RLU (Relative Luminescence Units) and the X-axis represents Log M (Molar concentration of antibody). Four neutralization curves are shown for each variant: hlgG1 ctrl (control), REGN3479 (maftivimab), REGN3471 (odesivimab), and REGN-EB3 (cocktail). In all three variants, REGN-EB3 shows the highest neutralization potency, followed by REGN3479, REGN3471, and hlgG1 ctrl.

The Y-axis for all plots is C_{max} (mg/L), ranging from 800 to 1600.

L2065: Figure 11 displays three scatter plots comparing the AUC_{inf} (Area Under the Curve extrapolated to infinity) in mg \cdot day/L for three different monoclonal antibodies (REGN3470, REGN3471, and REGN3479) across three groups: healthy human, infected NHP, and uninfected NHP. The Y-axis for all plots ranges from 0 to 30,000 mg \cdot day/L.
REGN3470 (AUC_{inf} vs. REGN3470):
Healthy human: Data points range from approximately 15,000 to 25,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Infected NHP: Data points range from approximately 5,000 to 8,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Uninfected NHP: Data points range from approximately 10,000 to 15,000 mg \cdot day/L.
REGN3471 (AUC_{inf} vs. REGN3471):
Healthy human: Data points range from approximately 20,000 to 30,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Infected NHP: Data points range from approximately 5,000 to 8,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Uninfected NHP: Data points range from approximately 10,000 to 15,000 mg \cdot day/L.
REGN3479 (AUC_{inf} vs. REGN3479):
Healthy human: Data points range from approximately 15,000 to 25,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Infected NHP: Data points range from approximately 5,000 to 8,000 mg \cdot day/L.
Uninfected NHP: Data points range from approximately 10,000 to 15,000 mg \cdot day/L.
In all three plots, the AUC_{inf} values for healthy humans are consistently higher than those for infected NHPs, and uninfected NHPs generally show intermediate values.
L2066: Figure 12 displays three scatter plots comparing the elimination half-life (t1/2) in days for REGN3470, REGN3471, and REGN3479 across three groups: healthy human, infected NHP, and uninfected NHP. The y-axis for all plots is t1/2 (day), ranging from 0 to 30. For REGN3470, healthy human half-lives are clustered around 25-30 days. Infected NHP half-lives are clustered around 5-10 days. Uninfected NHP half-lives are clustered around 15-20 days. Similar trends are observed for REGN3471 and REGN3479, showing distinct separation of half-lives based on the group.
L2067: Kaplan-Meier Curve for Survival, ITT Concurrent Population, PALM Trial. The plot shows Product-Limit Survival Estimates versus Analysis Value (0 to 60). The Y-axis is Survival Probability (0.0 to 1.0). The legend indicates two treatment arms: REGN-EB3 (solid blue line) and ZMapp (dashed red line). The REGN-EB3 arm shows a higher survival probability, starting around 0.75 at day 0 and stabilizing around 0.65 after day 20. The ZMapp arm starts around 0.95 at day 0 and drops sharply, stabilizing around 0.5 after day 20. A legend box indicates '+ Censored'.

Figure Description: This line graph displays the Mean Serum Sodium (mmol/L) by Study Day (Visit Number) for the REGN-EB3 and ZMapp arms in the Safety Population of the PALM Trial. The Y-axis ranges from 130 to 148 mmol/L. The X-axis ranges from Visit Number -1 to 28. Both arms show a similar trend, starting around 131 mmol/L at Visit -1, rising to a peak around Visit 7 (approximately 139.5 mmol/L), and then fluctuating between 137 and 140 mmol/L until Visit 25. After Visit 25, both arms show a sharp increase, peaking around Visit 25 (REGN-EB3 at approximately 143.5 mmol/L, ZMapp at approximately 138.5 mmol/L), followed by a decline towards Visit 28 (REGN-EB3 around 138 mmol/L, ZMapp around 136.5 mmol/L). Error bars represent the Mean (SE).

L2074: Bar chart showing Binding Response (nm) for three monoclonal antibodies (REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN3479) against three targets: 300 nM EBOV GP.10xhis (blue), 300 nM EBOV sGP.mmh (red), and 300 nM hCNTFR.mmh (green). REGN3470 and REGN3479 show high binding response (around 0.6 nm) to EBOV GP.10xhis. REGN3471 shows a moderate binding response (around 0.55 nm) to EBOV GP.10xhis. REGN3471 shows a significantly higher binding response (around 0.2 nm) to EBOV sGP.mmh compared to REGN3470 and REGN3479, which show minimal binding to EBOV sGP.mmh. All antibodies show minimal binding to the irrelevant control hCNTFR.mmh.
L2075: Western blot analysis showing the detection of antibody binding to EBOV GP.10xhis by REGN3470, REGN3471, and REGN3479. The blots show molecular weight markers (10 kDa to 150 kDa) and four lanes (1, 2, 3, 4) for each antibody. REGN3470 and REGN3471 show strong binding signals (around 150 kDa). REGN3479 shows a much weaker binding signal (around 150 kDa), indicating a lower affinity or different epitope conformation.
L2076: Stacked bar chart showing accumulative binding signals for three sequential mAb injections over the EBOV GP.10xhis surface in all permutations of the order of injection. The binding signal of the 50 µg/mL mAb injection is designated as: REGN3470 (blue); REGN3471 (red); and REGN3479 (green). The bottom being the first injection and the top the last injection. The Y-axis is Binding Response (RU), ranging from 0 to 250. The X-axis shows the sequence of injections: REGN3470-REGN3471-REGN3479, REGN3470-REGN3479-REGN3471, REGN3471-REGN3470-REGN3479, REGN3471-REGN3479-REGN3470, REGN3479-REGN3470-REGN3471, and REGN3479-REGN3471-REGN3470. All bars show a total response around 220-230 RU.

For REGN3471 ELISA Assay, REGN3471 mAb shows high inhibition (~90%), REGN3470 mAb shows moderate inhibition (~60%), REGN3479 mAb shows low inhibition (~30%), and the Cocktail shows high inhibition (~90%). For REGN3479 ELISA Assay, REGN3479 mAb shows high inhibition (~90%), REGN3470 mAb shows moderate inhibition (~60%), REGN3471 mAb shows low inhibition (~30%), and the Cocktail shows high inhibition (~90%). L2079: Figure 25: Neutralization of Ebola VLPs in Huh7 Cells. REGN3479 shows the highest neutralization activity, reaching approximately 100% neutralization at a Log M of -8. REGN3470 and REGN3471 show lower activity, reaching approximately 75% and 50% neutralization, respectively, at similar concentrations.
L2080: Figure 26 displays four graphs (A, B, C, D) showing the Fold Induction of Phagocytosis versus Concentration Log_{10} (M) for various antibodies.
A. No Blocking: REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN-EB3, and REGN3479 show concentration-dependent ADCP activity, reaching maximum fold induction (around 20-30 fold) at low concentrations (around 10^{-10} M). The IgG1 Isotype Control shows minimal activity.
B. Blocking with IgG1 anti-FCGR3A: REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN-EB3, and REGN3479 show significantly reduced ADCP activity, indicating that the activity is Fc\gammaR3A-dependent. The IgG1 Isotype Control shows minimal activity.
C. Blocking with IgG1 Isotype Control: REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN-EB3, and REGN3479 show concentration-dependent ADCP activity, similar to the No Blocking condition, indicating that the activity is not blocked by the IgG1 Isotype Control.
D. Rituximab (anti-CD20 IgG1): Rituximab shows strong ADCP activity (up to 60 fold). Rituximab activity is blocked by preincubation with IgG1 anti-FCGR3A, but not by preincubation with IgG1 Isotype Control.
Legend (applies to A, B, C):
REGN3470 (Circle)
REGN3471 (Square)
REGN-EB3 (Down Triangle)
REGN3479 (Up Triangle)
IgG1 Isotype Control (Star)
Legend (applies to D):
Rituximab + No Blocking (Circle)
Rituximab + Blocking with IgG1 anti-FCGR3A (Square)
Rituximab + Blocking with IgG1 Isotype Control (Up Triangle)

L2086: Figure 32 displays four Kaplan-Meier survival plots comparing the survival of guinea pigs exposed to Guinea Pig-Adapted Ebola Virus and treated with different monoclonal antibody (mAb) cocktail formulations. The X-axis represents Days (0 to 30), and the Y-axis represents Percent survival (0 to 100).
The four plots show survival curves for the following groups:
Top Left: REG3470/3471/3479 (Red line) and REGN3051 (Blue line).
Top Right: REGN3051 (Blue line) and REGN3051/3470/3471 (Green line).
Bottom Left: REGN3051 (Blue line) and REGN3051/3470/3479 (Purple line).
Bottom Right: REGN3051/3471/3479 (Black line) and REGN3051 (Blue line).
The REGN3051 group (Blue line) consistently shows the lowest survival rate across all plots, indicating that the nonreactive control mAb REGN3051 provided no protection against lethal infection. The other three groups (REG3470/3471/3479, REGN3051/3470/3471, and REGN3051/3471/3479) show significantly higher survival rates, demonstrating the protective effect of the mAb cocktails.

By D6, the 300 mg/kg dose shows a slight increase in titer (around 1.E+08.5), while the 150 mg/kg dose remains stable. By D8, the 300 mg/kg dose shows a significant decline in titer (around 1.E+05), while the 150 mg/kg dose shows a moderate decline (around 1.E+07).
L2108: Bar chart showing Mean EBOV Titer at Day 5 Prior to Treatment Among NHPs Treated With 150 mg/kg REGN-EB3. The Y-axis is EBOV Titer (GE/mL) on a logarithmic scale (1.E+07 to 1.E+10). The blue bar represents Survivors, showing a mean titer around 1.E+08. The orange bar represents Nonsurvivors, showing a mean titer around 1.E+09.
L2109: Bar chart showing Mean Day 5 Titers and sGP Concentrations of NHPs That Received 150 mg/kg. The chart compares Survivors and Nonsurvivors. For Survivors, Day 5 GE/mL (blue bar) is approximately 1.5 x 10^7, and Day 5 gGP (orange bar) is approximately 1.2 x 10^6. For Nonsurvivors, Day 5 GE/mL (blue bar) is approximately 1.2 x 10^8, and Day 5 gGP (orange bar) is approximately 4.5 x 10^7. The Y-axis for EBOV titer (GE/mL) is logarithmic, ranging from 1.0 x 10^5 to 1.0 x 10^9. The Y-axis for sGP (µg/mL) is linear, ranging from 0 to 60.
L2110: Figure 56 displays two graphs (A and B) showing the binding of REGN-EB3 monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) to Ebola Virus Glycoprotein (GP) expressed on Virus-Like Particles (VLPs), measured as Relative Light Units (RLU) versus concentration (M).
Graph A: Zaire 2014 EBOV GP
The Y-axis represents RLU (0 to 12000) and the X-axis represents Concentration (M) from -14 to -4. Three REGN-EB3 mAbs (REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN3479) and an IgG1 Isotype Control are plotted. REGN3470 and REGN3471 show high RLU values, reaching approximately 10,000 RLU. REGN3479 shows intermediate RLU values, reaching approximately 6,000 RLU. The IgG1 Isotype Control shows the lowest RLU values, reaching approximately 2,000 RLU.
Graph B: Zaire 2014 EBOV GP w/ T544I SNP
The Y-axis represents RLU (0 to 12000) and the X-axis represents Concentration (M) from -14 to -4. The binding curves for REGN3470, REGN3471, REGN3479, and the IgG1 Isotype Control are similar to Graph A, indicating that the T544I substitution does not significantly alter the binding affinity of the individual REGN-EB3 mAbs.
L2111: Figure 57 displays three graphs showing the effects of GP sequence variants (Zaire 2014, E280G, and E564K) on VLP neutralization. The Y-axis represents Relative Light Units (RLU) and the X-axis represents Log M (antibody concentration). Four antibodies are tested: REGN3479 (blue circles), REGN3471 (green triangles), REGN3470 (red squares), and REGN-EB3 (orange diamonds). In the Zaire 2014 graph, REGN3479 and REGN-EB3 show complete neutralization. In the E280G graph, REGN3479 and REGN-EB3 show concentration-dependent neutralization. In the E564K graph, REGN3479 and REGN-EB3 show concentration-dependent neutralization, while REGN3470 and REGN3471 show minimal or no neutralization.
